Treaty With Estonia on Troop Withdrawal Ratified

LD2107104995 Moscow ITAR-TASS in English
1001 GMT 21 Jul 95

[By Ivan Novikov]

{FBIS Transcribed Text] Moscow, July 21 (ITAR-
TASS) — The Sate Duma on Friday ratified a Russian-
Estonian tresy on the withdrawal of Russian armed
forces froin Estonia and conditions of their temporary
presence there.

The treaty was passed by 259 votes with two against
and two abstentions.

Russia had withdrawn its forces from Estonia by August
31, 1994, in strict compliance with the terms of the
treaty.

The State Duma also ratified a Russian-Estonian agree-
ment on social guarantees for Russian army pensioners
living in Estonia.

The work on the implementation of the treaty is not
simple, however the agreement keeps protecting the
Russian Federation even now, Russian Ambassador at
large Vasiliy Svirin said presenting the documents for
ratification.

The accords regard as “completely settled” all claims of
the Estonian party to Russia, including property issues,
Svirin said.

Ten thousand Russian pensioners and 30 thousands
members of their families live in Estonia. All articles
of the documents are aimed at protecting their rights,
the diplomat said.

Estonia has not ratified the documents so far, however
"Russia’s active stance on the issue may acts as a
good stimulus to Estonia in settling the issue in a
positive way," Chairman of the Duma committee for
international affairs Vladimir Lukin said.

On ratification, the Russian Finance Ministry will have

a legal basis for transferring pensions to Russian army
veterans in Estonia, Lukin said.

Duma Committee Chiefs Reply to Chernomyrdin

MM2107114895 Moscow SOVETSKAYA ROSSIYA
in Russian 20 Jul 95 pp 1-2

[Abridged account of 19 July State Duma speeches by
Aleksandr Chernyshev, chairman of the Duma Commit-
tee for Agrarian Questions; S. Glazyev, chairman of the

RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS 21

Committee for Economic Policy; M. Zadornov, chair-
man of the Committee for Budgets, Taxes, Banks, and
Finances; V. Ilyukhin, chairman of the Security Com-
mittee; and S. Kalashnikov, chairman of the Committee
for Labor and Social Support: "Duma Attacks Myths.
Premier V. Chernomyrdin Presents Report on the So-
cioeconomic Situation in the Country to Parliamentari-
ans" — first two paragraphs are ITAR-TASS report]

(FBIS Translated Text] Moscow, 19 July (ITAR-TASS)
— Viktor Chernomyrdin, head of the Russian Feder-
ation Government, today briefed the State Duma on
the socioeoconomic situation and the results of the
government’s work in the first half-year. According
to the prime minister, “the socioeconomic situation in
the country is taking on new features, although it re-
mains difficult, perhaps even very difficult, and some-
times contradictory.” However, as a whole, the premier
stressed, “we are seeing a recovery in several branches
and sectors of the economy.” Some signs of stabiliza-
tion in the Russian economy, in Chernomyrdin’s opin-
ion, testify not “to the nearness of prosperity," but to
strict adherence to the goals outlined by the president
and the government for 1995.

At the same time the government is especially con-
cerned by the causes of the slower than forecast rate of
economic recovery, Viktor Chernomyrdin stressed par-
ticularly. It is obvious today that the higher than ex-
pected growth of prices is the price which must be paid
for underestimating the real scale of the dollarization of
the economy, he said.

And here is how leaders of State Duma economic com-
mittees see the situation in the country. The speeches
are published with abridgment.

A. Chernyshev, chairman of the Committee on Agrarian
Questions:

We, the agrarian deputies, are concerned by the state
of affairs in the country’s economy, especially in the
agro-industrial complex, where around 40 million of our
fellow citizens live and work and where almost one-
third of our GDP is produced.

It can be said without exaggeration that the socioeco-
nomic position of our entire society, its spiritual con-
dition, and the political climate in many ways depend
upon the situation in this sector of the economy. How-
ever, today a Catastrophic situation is arising in the coun-
try with regard to food production.

The production of meat in the period of the beginning of
reforms decreased by 28 percent per head of population,
and that of milk by 30 percent. At the present time,
because of the high cost and insufficient supply of food

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

22 RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS

products, almost 45 million of our fellow citizens have
been cut off from normal consumption of these products.

Last year for the first time the country’s agriculture
ceased to be profitable. Sixty percent of all classes of
farms cannot carry out not only expanded, but even
simple reproduction.

I think everyone understands that in market conditions
loss-making production simply cannot exist, and that
the situation in the countryside is not only difficult, but
supremely difficult. It is impossible to feed a family on
the lowest wage of just over 100,000 rubles [R] and
given the six-month delay in receiving it. Hopelessness
and poverty are unfortunately a sad reality of rural life
today.

In the current year the situation is deteriorating still
further. The reason is that the disparity between the
prices of agricultural and industrial output and services
is literally obliterating the results of the rural laborers’
work. Whereas in 1991 peasants could buy two tonnes
of gasoline for every tonne of grain sold to the state,
last year it was only half a tonne.

The uncontrolled hiking of electricity prices (one kilo-
watt today now costs R140) has led to farms owing
hundreds of billions of rubles, and things are reach-
ing the point of absurdity. At the height of summer, in

some places, power supplies are cut off from milking
machines and from mechanized threshing floors at the
height of the harvest; irrigation systems do not work.

According to the estimates of the Russian Ministry
of Agriculture and Food, the agro-industrial complex’
losses from the disparity of prices total R60 trillion.
And at the same time, the state reduces federal budget
support for agricultural commodity producers from year
to year. Whereas in 1993 more than 7 percent of the
expenditure part of the federal budget was channeled
into the funding of the agro-industrial complex, in
1995 this share was only 4.5 percent. And in the draft
version of the 1996 budget it is even less. Allowing
for an inflation index of 1.32, R4 trillion worth of
budgetary appropriations are planned. Less than this
year. And this constitutes only 2.25 percent of budget
expenditure. Surely this cannot be spoken of with
equanimity? What were Ministers Panskov, Yasin, and
government First Deputy Chairman Chubays guided
by in introducing such provisions? Once again they
are striving to get their own way by petty sops and
concessions. The main obstacle to stabilizing and halting
the decline in agricultural production is the negligent
lack of commitment to fulfilling the federal budget...

In the present situation, whereby the state refuses to
allocate credits, agricultural production is supported

FBIS-SOV-95-141
24 July 1995

through budget funds. Then why, the question arises, has
funding of the sector been put in the hands of Ministry
of Finance functionaries, and not the government? Many
people want to know why the authorities in Moscow
are trying to finish off the agro-industrial complex with
their policies. Peasants are not to blame for the situation
in which they find themselves. Many of those present
here know what support the majority of foreign states
give to their commodity producers, shielding them from
market-driven prices with a system of grants, a regulated
parity of prices, and subsidies that our peasant has never
even dreamed of. After all, the decline in the fertility
of land and the destruction of the material-technical
and social basis of agriculture have reached the stage
where their restoration becomes problematic even given
financial resources....

If we avoid resolving the countryside’s problems, in
other words, fail to resolve the peasant question, there
can be no talk of stabilization in the economy. Boosting
agriculture means developing processing sectors, light
industry, machine building, and so forth. Russia has
always found the strength to escape from the most
difficult situations, and we hope that a way out of
today’s situation will be found also. The main thing is
that the agrarian sector of the economy should become
a priority, not in words, but in reality.

S. Glazyev, chairman of the Committee for Economic
Policy:

Talk of stabilization and of some success in the battle
with the slump in industrial production is a serious
delusion. The government reports that the slowdown in
the decline in output in the first half-year, especially
in June, is a sign of stabilization being achieved and
of transition to a new phase in economic policy. In
fact these figures are presented in comparison with the
depths to whicli we sank last summer. This was a
Catastrophic slump in industry at the level of more than
23 percent, and we were then told that it was simply
impossible to sink lower. In fact, the slump intensified
in the first half-year. The decline in the first quarter was
5 percent compared with the previous quarter, and in
the second quarter a further 4 percent. That is to say,
the slump is continuing, and this is the first thing that
needs to be said.

The trend toward the decline of the efficiency and qual-
ity of production is also continuing. De-industrialization
trends have intensified. If there is indeed a certain sta-
bilization in the raw material sectors, which are geared
toward exports, in the processing industry and in the
production of consumer goods, in particular in high-tech
industries, the slump remains very, very significant.

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

FBIS-SOV-95-141
24 July 1995

And the we-st thing of all is that the trend toward
declining living standards has intensified. If this trend
continues, the decline in consumer expenditure this year
could reach 7 percent. In the first two quarters alone
real pay per individual declined by almost a third. The
average per capita income of the disadvantaged stratum
of the population dropped 25 percent below the cost of
the minimum consumer basket.

Yesterday I returned from Arkhangelsk Oblast, and I
would like to report to my esteemed superiors that in
agricultural regions people are forced to feed their chil-
dren on cattle feed because their income is clearly in-
sufficient even to obtain bread and basic food products.

The critical situation in agriculture has already been
mentioned.

Now for a highly important government policy indicator
— the battle with inflation.

Despite the fact that the budget deficit has been substan-
tially reduced, inflation remains intolerably high. And
if the current trends are maintained, it will average at
least 7-8 percent per month, which is more than twice
the guide figure of which the government spoke.

The situation in the defense complex is extremely dif-
ficult. The government continues to underfund the de-
fense order. Literally yesterday I returned from Severod-
vinsk, where they cannot obtain payment for output al-
ready ordered and produced. This year work to the sum
of around R300-400 billion at one plant alone has not
been paid for. And the problem here is not demand. The
problem is that the government continues to fail to ful-
fill its obligations.

The investments crisis is intensifying. The slump in
investments in the first six months of the year was
more than 20 percent, and if the current trends persist,
the slump will be one-and-a-half times larger than the
guidelines which we arrived at when examining the
forecast for 1995.

Thus not one of the priority goals which were advanced
in the president’s message, the government’s program,
and in the forecast which we examined while ratifying
the budget has been fulfilled.

It is therefore very strange to hear from government
leaders about the presence of certain achievements and
changes for the better. I cannot help but say that many
negative phenomena are caused by outright mistakes
in the government’s activity. Above all, this concerns
the highly serious underfunding of all state expenditure.
The budget has been fulfilled mainly thanks to higher
inflation, and not through improving tax collection; real
expe" .*ure, or to be more precise, nominal expenditure

RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS 23

was underfunded by 15-20 percent, and in real terms,
allowing for inflation, by almost 40 percent. All this
has led to a very serious reduction in demand, and, as
a consequence, to the intensification of the industrial
slump.

I think the best thing that we can do today, having
examined the results of the first six months and the
planning indicators for 1996, is to pass a second vote
of no confidence in the government. We have three
months; they have not yet elapsed. According to the
documents which we have studied, it is obvious that
to all appearances no one has any particular intention
of correcting the mistakes of which we spoke 1 July.
Therefore, if we do not want to become accomplices te
the destruction of our country’s science and industrial
complex and its scientific and technical potential, I
believe that we have only one option.

If we lack the strength of character, I would propose that
we vote for the draft decree issued to deputies, in which
the government’s activity is deemed unsatisfactory.

M. Zadornov, chairman of the Committee on Budgets,
Taxes, Banks, and Finances:

What is the main result of the first half-year? In our view
— we have discussed this question — the main result is
that the goal which the government set itself for 1995,
to wit financial stabilization — this task has suffered
complete faiiure. Seventy-eight percent inflation in the
first half-year — that is more than in the first half of last
year, and almost twice as much as in the government’s
initial plans for the current year.

But in order to achieve this goal, a task which has
effectively failed, wide sections of society have made
considerable sacrifices. You know that the minimum
wage was not indexed from last July through March of
the current year. You know that in several sectors there
is a delay in the payment of wages of about two months,
and about 20 percent of this delay is connected with
funding from the federal budget. What has this led to?
It has led to a situation where, in comparison with last
June, pay has been reduced by 30 percent in real terms,
and the population’s real income by approximately 10
percent. In effect, this is the first such sharp decline in

V. Ilyukhin, chairman of the Security Committee:

The speeches by my committee chairmen colleagues
relieve me of the need to make a detailed analysis of the
state of the country’s national security. I will dwell only
on some aspects of it. But even they make it possible
to assert that Russia is facing the real danger that the
country will be turned into a new type of semicolony.
And this is hardly laying it on thick.

On 19 out of 20 main indicators the country long ago
fell into the critical danger zone, beyond which lies the
collapse of all society’s and the state’s security. This
is not only the opinion of our committee’s deputies but
also the Russian Academy of Sciences’ conclusions.

The situatiGu is so critical in all spheres of the economy,
social life, and defense capability that we have only
months, and not years left to rectify it and save Russia.
If, of course, we want the country to regain its status as
a great power.

Therefore I am worried by the somewhat placid, sooth-
ing tone of Viktor Stepanovich Chernomyrdin’s speech.
But there really is something to ponder.

In the last three and a half years Russia’s GDP has been
more than halved. Output has fallen by more than 60

percent in machine building and by 70 percent in light
industry.

The country is losing its general educational and scien-
tific potential, primarily in the military-industrial com-

While the world’s advanced states annually spend at
least 2 percent of appropriations from GDP on science,
in Russia this indicator was only 0.32 percent. The
country is steadily sliding into the abyss of darkness
and ignorance.

The living standard of almost 120 million Russians has
fallen by a factor of between three and five. Judging
from the prime minister’s report it will steadily get
worse, while the government still intends to build a
bright future for the minority of the country’s popu-
lation, to which the government itself belongs.

Russians’ social protection continues to decline. The
Stratification of society by income has exceeded the
maximum permissible norm for stable states. World
experience shows that a tenfold differential between the
incomes of the population’s richest and poorest strata
is critical and fraught with the danger of sociopolitical
upheavals.

Currently the gulf in Russia between the wage levels
of the lowest-paid 10 percent and the highest-paid 10
percent of workers has reached a factor of 27. Such a
phenomenon does not exist in any country throughout
the world.

According to official data the number of unemployed
people in the country, taking into account hidden un-
employment, is estimated at 13 percent, while accord-
ing to the assessments of our own and foreign analysts
the 8-10 percent mark is defined as dangerous.

Death from accidents, poisoning, and injuries is the
number one cause of death for Russians of working age.

And in this situation the government gives a negative
finding on the State Duma deputies’ draft law "On
Industrial Safety," offering virtually nothing instead.

This is to say nothing of the fact that it cannot curb
crime, which has become a national disaster for Russia,
and cannot ensure that Russians can live in safety. The
events in Budennovsk are a graphic illustration of this.

The lack of sufficient financial and material support
has practically wrecked the crimefighting program’s
implementation. Proper funding for law enforcement
organs and special services is not being ensured. I wish
I was mistaken, but during the past year to 18 months
the government has never once profoundly analyzed
or discussed the state of the fight against lawbreaking,

The production of basic food products has decreased by

In a number of regions Russians are already eating food

Over 50 percent of the food consumed, of extremely

The country’s food security is moving into first place

It has already led to a number of catastrophic conse-

While in European countries a life expectancy of 85
years is predicted for the population, in Russia a further

The demographic factor is being sharply exacerbated by

The population’s physical degradation is exacerbated by

S. Kalashnikov, chairman of the Committee for Labor
and Social Support:

Today we must work out how correct the line which

continuing it in the future? We intend to ratify a whole

In this connection I would like to turn your attention to

The first myth is connected with financial stabilization.
Mikhail Mikhaylovich Zadornov said that as of today
the figure for the first five months’ inflation totals
78 percent. And in 1994 it was 72 percent over the
first five months. That is, in other words, the declared
stabilization, which is the condition of a tough financial
policy, does not stand up to criticism. Yes, all budget-
funded enterprises and everyone who really depends on
the state feels the tough financial policy fully, but this
has nothing to do with inflation.

You wonder whether the government fundamentally
does not understand which sources of inflation deter-

The second aspect is connected with the government’s

That is, it is clear from the simplest figures that the

Duma Ratifies Agreements on Military Cooperation

LD2107172995 Moscow INTERFAX in English
1536 GMT 21 Jul 95

[FBIS Transcribed Text] Moscow, July 21 (INTER-
FAX) — On Friday Russia’s State Duma, the lower
house of the Russian parliament, ratified agreements on
Russia’s military cooperation with Tajikistan and Kyr-
gyzstan.

These decrees stipulate the legal status of Russian citi-
zens’ military service in the armed forces of Tajikistan
and Kyrgyzstan.

The agreements also provide for the functioning of
Russian military sites in these republics.

The matter, in particular, concerns the Nurek space
control system situated on Tajik territory. It helps to
the Russian air defense forces to detect and to identify
spacecraft flying in orbit at ranges of 2,000 to 40,000
kilometers. Russia has already allocated 86% of the
necessary funds to build this station. As many as 490
Russian servicemen serve at Nurek station.

The presence of the Russian military advisers in Tajik-
istan will give assistance in the formation of the re-
public’s own armed forces in order to gradually re-
place the Russian peacemaking contingent for the Tajik
one, Rocket Forces and Artillery Commander Nikolay
Dimidyuk reported to deputies.

Duma Adopts Bills on Military Issues

MM2107111495 Moscow KRASNAYA ZVEZDA
in Russian 21 Jul95 pl

[Report by Vladimir Yermolin: "The State Duma Cor-
rects Its Military Decisions")

[FBIS Translated Text] The State Duma has jacked
up the pace of law examination and adoption before
the approaching parliamentary vacations. On Thursday
morning the parliamentarians examined several draft
laws which have a direct bearing on the Russian
Federation Armed Forces.

Amendments to the Russian Federation law "On Mili-
tary Service Obligation and Military Service" — more
accurately, to the recently adopted law on making
amendments and additions to this law — were adopted
at once in three readings, i.e., on the whole. Thus the
increase in the term of service to two years does not ex-
tend to those who were drafted for 18 months. Further,
deferment from service has been restored for draftees

RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS 27

raised and maintained by a parent for at least seven
years, if the latter is over 50 years old, was not mar-
ried during the said period, and has no other children.
Finally, the term of service will be 18 months for those
who are drafted to “trouble spots" and who participate
in combat operations.

The whole question now is how the adopted amend-
ments will get past the Federation Council and the Rus-
sian Federation president.

The draft law "On Making Amendments and Additions
to the Russian Federation Law ‘On Defense’" got
through its first reading on the same day. It says, in
particular, that “the creation of military (paramilitary)
formations by components of the Russian Federation,
organs of local self-government, political parties, public
organizations, private and state enterprises, and also
citizens is prosecuted under the law." Part 3 of Article
10 is set forth as follows: "The involvement of units,
subunits, and other formations of the Russian Federation
Armed Forces in the fulfillment of tasks unconnected
with ensuring the defense of the Russian Federation is
permitted only on the basis of federal law." In the same
article: "It is forbidden to use the Russian Federation
Armed Forces and military formations of the Ministry
of Internal Affairs and the Federal Security Service
to resolve political conflicts inside the country." The
authors of the draft law attempted to make changes
to the Russian Federation Criminal Code providing for
punishment for the use of the Armed Forces on the
territory of the Russian Federation in excess of their
authority or official powers. The deputies reckoned that
the Russian Federation Criminal Code interprets this
point exhaustively even without additions.

Duma Rejects Cabinet’s Move To Change Budget

MK2207082895 Moscow SEGODNYA in Russian
21 Jul 95 p2

[Report by Svetlana Lolaeva: "Duma Refused To Untie
the Government’s Hands. Cabinet Has Already Granted
‘Extra’ Loans Worth 4.5 Trillions Rubles")

[FBIS Translated Text] The State Duma again took up
budget problems at its extraordinary sitting yesterday.
The law "On Quarterly Distribution of Federal Bud-
get Revenues and Expenditures for 1995" was passed
on its first reading in spite of several critical remarks
by the Budget Committee — in particular, on the un-
equal distribution of budget expenditures. The Duma,
however, rejected government-presented draft laws in-
troducing changes and amendments to the 1995 Budget
Law.

According to Mikhail Zadornov, the Budget Committee
chairman, the most important of these laws, which

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying .nd dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

28 RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS

consists of only two articles, grants the government
the right to spend additional budget revenues at its
discretion and to sequester expenditures. In the opinion
of the Budget Committee chairman, this formula "is
completely unacceptable to the Duma." The Committee
insists on specific changes being made to the budget,
giving a clear breakdown of the expenditures and an
indication of their precise purpose and the amount
sequestered, as the law requires.

Mr. Zadornov said that the preliminary results of the
six-month budget implementation have already come
in and they lead to fairly interesting conclusions. This
concerns budget loans in particular. Loans worth 2.5
trillion rubles, which should have been paid back
into the budget, remained at enterprises and in the
regions. Moreover, further loans valued at 4.5 trillion
rubles were offered, which were not stipulated in the
budget; in other words, some expenditures are being
used for purposes not accounted for by the 1995
Budget Law. The changes and amendments to the 1995
Budget Law proposed by the government, in Mikhail
Zadornov’s view, however, cover all these violations
and "will untie the government’s hands" in the future.
The Budget Committee will reconsider the draft laws
presented by the government next week and will make
its recommendations. For its part, the Duma, as the
Budget Committee chairman said, will be taking up the
issue of budget implementation more than once before
the end of this year.

CIS states and developing wide-range specialization and
cooperation,” the document said.

needs a concept of its involvement in European and
Asian Pacific integration groups.

The document singles out Russia’s joining GATT
(WTO) as one of the main goals today.

Duma Dissatisfied With Economic Performance

LD2107144195 Moscow INTERFAX in English
1153 GMT 21 Jul 95

{[FBIS Transcribed Text] MOSCOW, July 21 (INTER-
FAX) — The State Duma has passed a resolution on
Prime Minister Viktor Chernomyrdin’s report, presented
on July 19, on the socioeconomic situation in the coun-
try.

The resolution says that the government has failed to
implement the basic provisions of the medium-term
economic program for 1995-97, meaning that its work
in the first half of the year has been unsatisfactory.

The State Duma also emphasized that certain construc-
tive efforts made by the government for stabilizing the
economic situation are insufficient to reach the goals
listed in the presidential message to the Federal Assem-
bly.

The State Duma called on the president to "consider the
issue of personal responsibility of government leaders
for unsatisfactory implementation of the tasks outlined
in the presidential message.”

Parliament suggested that the government make good
on its debts to various sectors of the economy before
September 1 and that it extend a state loan of 2.7 trillion
rubles for purchases of grain and other farm products.

Furthermore, it urged the government to protect the
Russian domestic market from unfair competition and
take effective steps to collect the harvest.

The resolution was carried by 229 votes, while 226 votes
were needed for it to pass.

Federation Council Rejects Law on Electoral Map

LD2107203895 Moscow INTERFAX in English
1836 GMT 21 Jul 95

[FBIS Transcribed Text] Moscow, July 21 (INTER-
FAX) — The Federation Council, the upper house of
the Russian parliament, rejected a law on the electoral
map Friday as it disagreed with a number of its provi-
sions.

Only 66 deputies voted in favor of the document passed
by the State Duma, the lower house, on July 14, while

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

FBIS-SOV-95-141
24 July 1995

25 voted against and 5 abstained from voting. As many
as 90 votes are required to pass a decision.

The Federation Council proposed creating a joint con-
ciliatory commission with the State Duma to finalize the
law.

Meanwhile, article 11 of the electoral law stipulates that
a special law, due to be published 110 days before the
election day, will define the electoral map. Therefore,
such a law should be subsequently approved by the State
Duma, Federation Council and president before August
27, since the parliamentary elections were scheduled for
December 17.

Otherwise, the elections will be held in the same
constituencies as in December 12, 1993.

According to Central Election Commission Secretary
Vladimir Vishnyakov, in this case the election results
may be questioned because of a considerable difference
in the number of electors in various constituencies.
The new electoral law stipulates that the maximum
acceptable difference must be below 10% within the
region and below 15% in barely accessible and remote
areas.

[By ITAR-TASS parliamentary correspondent Lyud-

{FBIS Translated Text] Moscow, 21 Jul (ITAR-TASS)
— An agreement "On the international and legal guar-
antees for the unhindered and independent implemen-
tation of the activities of the Mir Interstate Television
and Radio company” was ratified today by the Federa-

The committee for affairs of the Commonwealth of
Independent States noted that “the main aim of the
activity of the Mir company is to cover objectively
and impartially all aspects of life and also international
ties between the founder-states of this company and to
inform citizens of events and processes taking place in
the Commonwealth". The agreement which was ratified
today will help the Mir television and radio company to
carry out this task in line with international law.

Official Minimizes Accord Significance

LD2107192195 Moscow Radio Rossii Network
in Russian 1800 GMT 2] Jul 95

(FBIS Translated Text] On Friday evening Marina
Chernukha interviewed Vladimir Zorin, first deputy
head of the Territorial Directorate of Federal Bodies of
Executive Power in Chechnya.

[Begin recording] [Chernukha] As is known, the ques-
tion of the status of Chechnya was discussed today and
judging by the satisfied faces of the participants in the
talks the correspondents concluded that the question had
already been resolved. What is your opinion about that?

{Zorin} Questions will be resolved when protocols or
documents are signed. The question of status is, of

course, a very important one but I would not attach to it
any more significance than it has. The fact of the matter
is that the delegations in their present composition are,
of course, unable to resolve that problem. It is not in
their power. Higher powers are needed. But the most
important thing is that approaches to this problem are
being sought and are perhaps being found.

(Chernukha] What do you think is still to be resolved
at this level in Groznyy during these talks?

{Zorin}] I think that quite a few important questions
have still to be discussed, for instance: Guarantees
for ensuring that the decisions on the military bloc
of issues are implemented, problems of disarmament,
guarantees on the non-use of force to resolve moot
political questions; and the most important thing —
guarantees for those involved in the conflict who have
not committed criminal offenses and who will stop
participating in the armed conflict voluntarily or by
mutual agreement. The question of a mechanism for
disarmament, a mechanism for elections guaranteeing
the free expression of the will of the peoples on the
most important questions of interest to society in the
Chechen Republic is evidently still be to discussed. [end

No Headway Viewed in Chechnya Status

’ MK2207120695 Moscow KOMMERSANT-DAILY

in Russian 22 Jul 95 p 3

[Commentary by Dmitriy Kamyshev: "Situation in
Chechnya. Talks Are Going Successfully, But Without
Results")

{[FBIS Translated Text] The intricacy of the Groznyy
peace talks is growing increasingly incomprehensible
to outside observers. The Russian side keeps speaking
about full agreement between the sides and imminent
signing of a final document, whereas the Chechens
continue insisting that this is still a distant prospect. The
difference in the assessments of progress in the dialogue
is SO great that at times one gets the impression that each
side is speaking about totally different talks.

After the resumption of the third round the custom-
arily upbeat statements on the course of the talks by
members of the Russian delegation have grown even
more optimistic. On Thursday [20 July] evening, Vyach-
eslav Mikhaylov [Nationalities Minister] declared that
“a compromise has virtually been found" and the sides
agreed on almost the entire section of the final document
relating to Chechnya’s status, save four lines. Yester-
day [21 July] morning Arkadiy Volskiy described the
formula found on Thursday "fairly acceptable for all"
and said that “unless the opposite side thinks up some-
thing additionally,” the talks may wind up on Satur-
day [22 July]. The greatest optimist of all proved to be
the vice premier in the Khadzhiyev government, Abdula
Bugayev, who assumed that the present round could end
as early as Friday.

However, the “opposite side" was for some reason un-
aware of the latest negotiating success. One of the Du-
dayev delegation negotiators, Akhyad Idigov, accused
Russian delegates, the day before, of retreating from the
already agreed positions following their recent consulta-
lions in Moscow. Speaking about the compromise on the
formula for the republic’s status, allegedly reached on
Thursday, he stated that he did not know what this for-
mula was. True enough, Idigov became familiar with the
Russian version by yesterday morning and said that the
wording proposed by the Russians “completely denies
the Chechen people the right to sovereignty,” whereas
all the statements by the Moscow delegates on an agreed
version of the document represent “planned misinfor-
mation" with the aim of accusing the Dudayevites of
being unyielding and disrupting the talks. The Chechen
delegate was also skeptical about the anticipated speedy
signing of an agreement. In his words, the Russian dele-
gation “is deliberately seeking a situation whereby some
fanatical group will repeat the Budennovsk tragedy in

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

FBIS-SOV-95-141
24 July 1995

some other place after the talks are driven into dead-
lock."

Such polar interpretations of how the talks are going
suggest that at least one of the sides is informing
journalists not too precisely, to put it mildly, on what
is happening in the OSCE mission building. Whereas
the Russian delegation is virtually unanimous in its
excessive optimism, Akhyad Idigov in the Dudayev
delegation largely plays the role of the main expe -t of
Moscow’s intrigues. One can of course assume ‘nat it
is he who represents the "hawks" seeking to disrupt the
peace dialogue repeatedly referred to by Mikhaylov and
Volskiy, whereas Usman Imayev and Aslan Maskhadov
in fact agree with Moscow on all matters. If we recall,
however, that none of the numerous Russian statements
on complete agreement between the sides and on a
final document to be signed the day after have been
confirmed, the unrestrained optimism of the Moscow
delegation appears much less realistic than the gloomy
pessimism of the Dudayevites.

talks.

Dudayev Says Talks success ‘Unlikely’

LD2307084395 Moscow ITAR-TASS World Service
in Russian 0751 GMT 23 Jul 95

{By Aleksandr Kharchenko and Sharip Asuyev]

[FBIS Translated Text] Groznyy, 23 July (ITAR-TASS)
— "These talks have not achieved anything worthwhile
and are unlikely to, because the are in the hands of
petty-minded fools," was how Dzhokhar Dudayev de-
scribed the Chechen peace talks continuing in Groznyy.
He spoke on his underground, so-called "presidential"
television channel, the transmissions of which were bro-
ken off after federal troops took Vedeno but resumed a
week ago.

Dudayev said he was skeptical about the Groznyy peace
talks because the Russian delegation is interested, as
he put it, not in establishing peace and tranquillity in
Chechnya but in prolonging the crisis.

Concerning the problem of the Chechen republic’s sta-
tus in state law, Dzhokhar Dudayev said: “The Chechen
Republic of Ichkeria is a sovereign independent state,
and the Chechen nation is a free people...”

Dudayev called on his fellow countrymen to give up the
illusion that Russia will restore anything in Chechnya,
but said there are other states and peoples ready to
provide Chechnya with real assistance. "We will have
new towns built of white stone, new roads, and new
communications!" he assured them.

In conclusion, Dzhokhar Dudayev said “there are no
forces on earth that will break the wil! of the Chechen
people and get it back into Russia’s animal stall."

Immediately after Dudayev’s interview, the same chan-
nel showed television interviews with Chechen field
commanders. The main theme emphasized in their state-
ments was that none of them needs war, but that if they
have to they will be ready together with their fighters
to die for the freedom and independence of Chechnya.

Chechen Delegate Views Status Problem

LD2307123995 Moscow INTERFAX in English
1159 GMT 23 Jul 95

([FBIS Transcribed Text] Groznyy, July 23 (INTER-
FAX) — According to Akhmed Zakayev, a member
of the Chechen delegation, at the talks in Groznyy of
great importance is the fact that today the Chechen del-
egation is not authorized to determine Chechnya’s re-
lations with the Russian Federation, while the Russian
delegation has no powers to recognize Chechnya’s in-
dependence."

Answering the questions put by INTERFAX, Zakayev
said: "I believe a neutral solution will be found and
we shall still sign an agreement on a block of political
questions."

Later in the day, he said, the two sides continued to
discuss "principally important political problems, which
are a stumbling block at the talks.”

Nevertheless, he said, "I do not think that an agreement
on political problems will be signed by the evening on
Sunday. This depends on many factors."

"I believe that the fact that we have put an end to
hostilities in Chechnya is the greatest achievement at
the talks today," and added: "We hope that the fighting
will not resume no matter whether we shall reach an
agreement on political questions or not.”

Negotiators View Obstacles

LD2407124295 Moscow ITAR-TASS World Service
in Russian 1200 GMT 24 Jul 95

[By Sharip Asuyev and Aleksandr Kharchenko]

{FBIS Translated Text] Groznyy, 24 July (ITAR-TASS)
— The main problem at the talks on a peaceful
settlement of the conflict in Chechnya remains the
formula on the status of Chechnya, the participants in
the talks who came out of the OSCE building where
the talks are taking place confirmed in a conversation
to journalists during a short break today.

At the same time, Vyacheslav Mikhaylov, head of
the Russian delegation, stressed: "It is not the status
of Chechnya which is at issue. Its status has been
determined. The Chechen Republic is a component
of the Russian Federation. What is at issue are the
characteristics of this status."

Vyacheslav Mikhaylov said that the Dudayev side does
not yet accept this formula. However, these talks are
aimed at overcoming precisely this and other differ-
ences, he stressed.

In the opinion of Vyacheslav Mikhaylov what is most
important is to form as soon as possible a unified body
which would have power over the entire territory of the
republic. The Dudayev side agrees to this, he noted.

Arkadiy Volskiy, a member of the Russian delegation,
told journalists that the situation at the talks had
become exacerbated after Dzhckhar Dudayev’s address
on underground television in Groznyy.

"Difficult and exhausting work is under way and will
continue," Arkadiy Volskiy stressed. He asked journal-
ists not to dramatize the situation.

Speaking next to journalists, Usman Imayev, head of
the Dudayev delegation, stressed that questions of the
status of Chechnya and also the place and role of the
legitimate bodies of power of the Chechen Republic are
the main problems at the talks.

Cossacks Urge Resettlement of Local Chechens

MM72407124695 Moscow IZVESTIYA in Russian
22 Jul 95 p2

[Report by Igor Rotar: "Cossack Time Bomb"]

[FBIS Translated Excerpt] Groznyy-Naurskiy Rayon-
Moscow — "We are all Chechen hostages," Nikolay
Kamyshnikov, hetman of Ishcherskaya Village on the
left bank (the Trans-Terek) side of Chechnya, said. The
Situation in this northern region of Chechnya remains
extremely tense. In the rayon center of Naur a rally of
local Chechens calling for the "withdrawal of occupying
troops" continues. "Why are you doing nothing, you
men! Take up arms and drive out the Russian soldiers!"
one of the speakers, a pretty girl of around 20, urged
the demonstrators.

Local Cossack meetings are voicing quite the opposite
demands — "return Shelkovskiy and Naurskiy Rayons
to Stavropol Kray, and resettle all the local Chechens
to the right bank." [passage omitted]

The hetman believes that the problem can only be solved
by radical means — “the left bank should be given
to Stavropol Kray, and all the Chechens should be
resettled. It is just impossible to reach agreement with
them. You know, our grandfathers used to joke that the
only good Chechen’s a dead Chechen!"

Need it be said that such a solution does not suit the
Chechens, who, incidentally, outnumber the Cossacks
on the left bank. "This is our land. If the Cossacks
want to live here they have to recognize that they are
in Chechnya," the Chechens believe.

But the villagers themselves believe that might is clearly
not on their side at the moment. "The Chechens have
very many real weapons, we just have a few hunting

FBIS-SOV-95-141
24 July 1995

rifles. We can only rely on help from our brother
Cossacks!" Esaul Mikhail Chebotarev told me.

Naurskiy Rayon borders Stavropol Kray, where in the

The government program for the stabilization of the

nomic regulation of the agrarian market as the priority
task. Gennadiy Romanenko, president of the Russian
Academy of Agricultural Sciences, said in Moscow that
this regulation is based on the provision of incentives
to ensure that the country is self-sufficient in terms of
basic foodstuffs.

The program envisages budget funding for the agro-
industrial complex amounting to at least 15 percent of
federal and regional budget spending. The task of raising
overall capital investment from all sources of funding by
the year 2000 to 155 trillion rubles [R], in other words
restoring the 1991 level, is being raised.

Russian Vice Premier Aleksandr Zaveryukha visited
a number of grain and livestock farms in the central
region.

The problems of peasants’ survival in impending market
conditions boil down to lack of money and fuel and low
prices of grain, meat, fruit, and vegetables. The vice pre-
mier was told this by private farmers, kolkhoz workers,
and farm leaders. In their view, the government must
place firm orders for deliveries of national foodstuffs to
local and federal stocks and enable peasants to provide
the country and themselves with a decent food supply.

There is no comprehension in the State Duma and
society as a whole that land forms the basis of our
prosperity. This was said by Russian Vice Premier
Aleksandr Zaveryukha when speaking to tractor drivers
in the field in Tambov Oblast’s Nikiforovskiy Rayon.
He claims that only a small number of deputies uphold
rural interests in the Duma.

You could see from the vice premier’s speech that
the Russian Government has no intention of providing
endless support for weak farms. He urged peasants
to rely on their own reserves. To begin with, they
must dramatically cut back the bureaucratic apparatus
at collective farms and rayon and oblast structures. It

is also necessary to dramatically reduce the number

{[FBIS Transcribed Text] Vladikavkaz, July 23 (ITAR-

statement that the Prigorodnyy district of North Ossetia
should be returned to Ingushetia.

In an article titled "Presidents Have No Claims" and
published in the July 13-19 issue of the OBSHCHAYA

did not and do not have any territorial claims.... As for

Soviet of the Russian Federation."

"No one has repealed that law yet," he noted.

in the republic because it "does not fit into" the
agreement signed by the presidents of the two republics
in Vladikavkaz.

In the agreement the presidents reiterated their commit-
ment to the Constitution of the Russian Federation and
confirmed that they had no territorial claims on each
other.

Aushev’s statement published by OBSHCHAYA
GAZETA runs counter to the productive ideas laid
out in the agreement which has become a new step
towards establishing normal relations between the two
neighbouring republics, the adviser said.

Criminal Case Against City Figures Related

954F2838A Moscow ROSSIYSKAYA GAZETA
in Russian 14 Jul95 p8

[Article by Viktor Yevseyev: "Sobchak’s Deputy Was
a Bit of a Thief. He Spawned Golden Caviar and
Trotted the C:obe. Criminal Charges Filed Against High
Ranking Bureaucrats From St. Petersburg")

[FBIS Translated Text] The St. Petersburg Procuracy
has sent to the city court a criminal case involving
a former deputy of the mayor of St. Petersburg, two
workers of the city customs office, and two other
residents of the city. This was reported by the Center
for Information and Public Relations of the General
Procuracy of the Russian Federation. They are accused

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

FBIS-SOV-95-141
24 July 1995

of illegally shipping caviar abroad. Additionally, the
former deputy mayor was mixed up in transactions with
foreign exchange assets—metal of the platinum group
osmium-187. In turn, the customs officials were charged
with official forgery and abuse of their office.

The procuracy established that the deputy of the chief of
the city of St. Petersburg, who was employed previously
in the trade administration of the Leningrad Oblast
Executive Committee and then as general director of the
Alisa trading house, had extensive contacts in trade and
commerce. He had foreign exchange accounts in foreign
banks and also three personal companies in Canada,
Belgium, and Germany. In the FRG there was a firm
called Fart-Dial, one of whose founders was, again, the
deputy mayor and one of the others involved in the
criminal case.

Procuracy investigators have been keeping track of
the illegal actions since the spring of 1993, when
the wheeler-dealers learned from the director of the
individual private enterprise Mercury that there was
a possibility of getting their hands on a considerable
amount of caviar and selling it for a profit abroad. The
only problem was that the business was kept from going
forward by a so-called special policy for exporting fish
and fish products. Without a license, the road to the
other side of the border was closed off to them. And
since they did not want to pass up a chance to make a
lot of easy money, they decided to go against the law
and send the caviar to Germany as contraband.

To do this, the bunch of scoundrels first assigned
the roles: Who was to acquire it, who was to look
for a buyer, who was to organize storage, and also
find an office for customs documentation and direct
transportation of the delicacy to the West. The wheels
began to turn. In the summer of 1993, two tonnes of
caviar, worth 9 million of those same rubles, were
successfully shipped to the address of Fart-Dial in
Berlin.

The appetite develops while eating. .he second batch
of the popular product was acquired and documented
with the participation of new wheeler-dealers. The
newcomers turned out to be valuable personnel, since
they had an extremely useful acquaintance—the deputy
chief of the personnel division of the St. Petersburg
customs office. He was the one who suggested declaring
the costly caviar as butter. Additionally, he accepted
the obligation of filling out false customs and other
documents for shipping the goods out of Russia.

The gallant personnel officer did not have access to the
customs stamps and seals. But he quickly managed to
involve in the crime one of the inspectors of the customs

RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS 39

freight department, who in a flash stamped two whole
sets of the necessary papers.

Hundreds of jars of caviar disguised as butter set off for
their respective destinations. But at the Velikoiukskaya
customs office, the vigilant guardians of Russia’s eco-
nomic interests halted the suspicious jars.

The deputy mayor then came up with a move that could
quickly rectify his financial position. With assistance
from one of his friends, he suggested selling abroad
very valuable and rare and strategically important raw
material, osmium-187.

The position of the deputy chief of the city enabled him
to obtain as an experimental sample 8 grams of this
metal, which had been produced on instructions from
the government of St. Petersburg at one of the city’s
enterprises. In 1995 this metal "brought" almost a half
billion rubles.

The deputy mayor did not register the osmium-187, as
he was supposed to, but turned it over to his dealer to
send it abroad and subsequently sell it. But there was
another slip-up—when entering Finland the unlucky
partner got caught.

The end of the affair is near. The St. Petersburg Procu-
racy has completed its investigation. Now there will be
a judicial hearing, and sentences for the contrabandists
who until recently held high-ranking positions.

Domestic Murders Fall, Contracts Rise in Moscow

LD2207172395 Moscow INTERFAX in English
1501 GMT 22 Jul 95

{[FBIS Transcribed Text] Moscow, July 22 (INTER-
FAX) — The Moscow Main Interior Department has
told INTERFAX that it registered a considerable growth
of contract killings (by nearly 20 percent) in Moscow
over the first half of 1995.

If 77 such cases had been registered in the first half of
1994, then their number in the first six months of 1995
reached 92. Businessmen were killed in 29 cases, while
the rest were mafia showdowns.

In January through June 1995, the number of deliberate
murders in Moscow went up to 861 against 842 over
the same period of 1994. At the same time, the number
of domestic murders reduced by 23 percent.

The Moscow police described as a "good result" the
higher rate of solved deliberate murders over the given
period.

At the same time, contract ones are much more difficult
to solve due to the killers’ high level of training and
equipment.

(By correspondent Gennadiy Kipkeyev]

{(FBIS Translated Text] Nalchik, 22 Jul (ITAR-TASS)
— Extra measures are being taken in Kabardino-
Balkaria to strengthen law and order on the territory
of the republic.

In line with today’s edict by Valeriy Kokov, president
of the Kabardino-Balkaria Republic, those wishing to
pass across the administrative frontier of the Kabardino-
Balkaria Republic may do so only on production of a
pass. This is a method for monitoring systematically
transit traffic, for inspecting and registering it. The rights

from the territory of Kabardino-Balkaria those with no
legal reason to be there.

These measures aimed at ensuring the safety of the
citizens of the republic will be implemented on a
temporary basis until the situation in the north of the
Caucasus region returns to normal, the edict stresses.

Theft of Nonferrous Metals in Novosibirsk Viewed

954F2839A Novosibirsk VECHERNIY NOVOSIBIRSK
in Russian 12 Jul95 p2

[Article by Irina Timofeyeva: "A Gold Mine for
Thieves. Sixty Tonnes of Copper, Aluminum, and
Other Nonferrous Metals Have Been Stolen in Novosi-
birsk During the Half-Year"]

([FBIS Translated Text] One fine May night, to the
singing of nightingales at the plant for reinforced
concrete items in Sovetskiy Rayon, a curious incident

FBIS-SOV-95-141
24 July 1995

occurred. The victim was a silent tower crane, which
was deprived, shall we say, of its main artery, namely
120 meters of electric cable. The police patrol arrested
the perpetrator out in an open field as he burned the
covering, trying to get to the copper core of the cable.
Or it would be better to call it gold. Because nonferrous
metals have become a true gold mine for some of our
compatriots.

The cables were cut from 22 cranes: from the power
panel to the top, as far as the culprits could climb. After
all, cable is an extremely complicated creation of human
hands, which has a multitude of strands and coverings.
But only one thing was important to these rogues—cut
it, burn it, and take it to the receiving point. Nonferrous
metal is being bought there at a price of from 3,000
to 6,000 per kilogram. If it is stolen (the workers at
the receiving point have a good eye for this), then it is
cheaper.

They make off with pure copper as well. From the
warehouse of the Etro plant in Dzerzhinskiy Rayon,
after breaking down the door, they stole more than two
tonnes of copper sheets. Six copper slabs were taken
right out of the shop at the Sibelektroterm Joint-Stock
Company. From the Elsib Joint-Stock Company—the
experimental plant—they took 30 copper rods. At our
former large plants that are now joint-stock companies,
such "losses," alas, are nothing unusual. But try to ask
the leaders, from the shop to the very highest level,
how this happens, why public property is disappearing,
and nobody knows for sure. Just as they were unable
to protect it during Soviet times, apparently this is now
a part of our genetic makeup: Public property means
it belongs to nobody or, conversely, it is mine. Police
Statistics show that about 40 percent of the thefts of
nonferrous metals are committed by individuals who are
employed at the enterprises.

Oh, these lovers of nonferrous metals should be sent to
Norilsk. If only they could see how much work goes
into copper plating and copper conductors! The miners
go down 2,000 meters from 40 degrees below zero to
40 degrees above. The combine where they produce
metal from the ore from a distance reminds one of the
netherworld—a gigantic cloud of smoke, gas, and steam.
Well, here too everything is for sale! The receiving
Clerk sells goods to the wholesalers at twice as much
as he paid for them from some random dockworker.
After that, the price increases geometrically, but still
stolen Russian nonferrous metals are considerably less
expensive abroad than the ones to be had there.

More than 200 thefts were reported during the half-year,
an increase of almost 90 percent as compared to the
same period of last year. 158 cases involved the theft

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

FBIS-SOV-95-141
24 July 1995

of copper, including 116 of copper cable. Thieves are
especially active in Dzerzhinskiy, Zayeltsovskiy, Lenin-
skiy, and Kirovskiy Rayons. A total of 60 tonnes of
nonferrous metals, worth 2 billion rubles, were stolen.
They steal and cut up everything. Transformer substa-
tions, telephone and electric communications lines, ele-
ments of signalization equipment, block systems, energy
sources for railroads.

It seems that until recently this situation disturbed
mainly the police, and certainly not the owners of the
nonferrous metals. Well, for example, on the night of 2
May a cable was stolen from the timber combine, and
the report on it was received by the Leninskiy Rayon
internal affairs administration in the mail on 11 May.
On 3 May, 45 kilograms of aluminum wire disappeared
from the warehouse of bridge train-429, and the report
arrived at the police station a week later. When taking
on-the-spot measures, the criminal investigation workers
find nonferrous metals that are not accounted for at the
warehouses.

There are severai reasons for the theft. The poor
guard protection, the open, unprotected areas (who, for
example, was protecting the tower crane?), and the
lack of supervision of receiving points, commercial
procurement structures, and private individuals — and
— the inefficiency of the owners. You will agree that
it is not easy to find something that the owner is trying
to protect. That is why fewer than 30 percent of these
crimes are solved.

“Why am I disturbed by the fact that our assets are
going abroad at a mark-down, when the owners are
not very upset?" asks the deputy chief of the criminal
investigation department of the oblast internal affairs
administration, Nikolay Zhurin. "We give them orders,
but they do not follow them! Sometimes reports of thefts
come in weeks or even months after the fact. They
say that they have no means of protection. Let them
calculate the losses caused by the theft of a simple crane.
No, just as they have not been able to figure it out,
they have not been able to learn: An automotive crane
was used to load nine coils of aluminum wire into two
Kamaz’s — and nothing happened. This is not the only
case. Now the fine for such a disgrace may be up to 20
times the minimum wage for the manager — almost a
million. But for this kind of negligence I would arrest
them, for a week or a month."

Perhaps that is too harsh, but it is very sad to see how
Russia is being robbed. The appeal from the police,
the procuracy, and the leaders of the enterprises has
produced results. Effective 1 July, strict orders from the
head of the oblast administration have gone into effect.
The office of the mayor of Novosibirsk must within

RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS 41

two months take stock at all receiving points. The tax
inspectorate, the internal affairs administration, and the
control-audit administration will inspect them carefully.
Now the receiving point does not reveal who has ended
up there and how. If it operates 24 hours a day, which
is not prohibited, the service to the clients should be
provided for up to 16 hours. That is, there should be
no night visits with stolen metal! A procedure has been
determined for receipt with an entry in a registry that
has been numbered and stamped by the police, where
all the information on the supplier is registered along
with the name of the scrap metal and its origin. A cash
book is also mandatory. Erasures and corrections are not
allowed in it or in the register. A license and registration
are needed. Only an officially appointed receiving clerk
may work at the point, and he may not be replaced by
another individual. Effective i July, there will be harsh
punishment for violation of these and other rules, right
down to closing of the receiving point. Will the flow of
our metal abroad come to a halt?

Situation at Kolyma Gold Mines Viewed

MM2107142295 Moscow RABOCHAYA TRIBUNA
in Russian 21 Jul95 p5

{Report by Vladimir Androsenko: "Glitter and Poverty
of Kolyma"}

(FBIS Translated Text] The prospecting season is in
full swing in Kolyma. The first kilograms of 1995 gold
have arrived at mine reception centers from mining and
enrichment combines.

The "Severovostokzoloto"” Joint-Stock Company mining
enterprise has turned out to be as well prepared as it
was last year. Admittedly, that should not be used as a
yardstick, since the miners obtained an advance payment
of 25 percent of their mining quota last year. They had
to abandon many traditional types of preparatory work
and focus their efforts on the most promising areas.

This year too the government has failed to keep its
promise to advance the gold industry 50 percent of
the value of its planned gold output. By the beginning
of the mining season, only around 32 percent had
been allocated. Moreover, extraction of underground
sands has almost halved — from 430,000 cubic meters
to 250,000. Mines containing around 1 gram of gold
per tonne have been closed down. They were simply
unprofitable. We would note in passing that such
deposits are pretty profitable in the rest of the world.
So the planned reduction of 2-3 tonnes in the mining of
the metal is entirely understandable.

The situation is slightly better with sands extracted
using open-cut methods. Usually out of a total of

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

42 RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS

80 million cubic meters of peat that is extracted, 50
million is extracted by the start of the mining season.
These parameters have been adhered to this year as
well. So there will be no decline in extraction from
alluvial deposits. The level of ore processing is being
maintained as well. On the whole, this will allow
"Severovostokzoloto” to obtain its forecast 20 tonnes
of precious metal. Alternative structures — prospecting
artels and joint-stock companies formed from disbanded
geological expeditions — intend to mine around 7
tonnes. The Magadan Oblast Administration is placing
great stress on independent prospectors. But so far its
hopes have not been borne out. Last year, for instance,
more than 1,500 independent prospectors worked in
Kolyma, but they produced just 90 grams of gold each.
Knowing local conditions, it is hard to believe those
production figures. Clearly, the rest of their output
found its way “under the counter" to the underground
"Ingushzoloto" syndicate.

Despite the legislators’ promises, no law on the precious
metals market has yet been adopted. As a result, gold
is still not a commodity. The miners put many of their
problems down to this fact.

Admittedly, last year the government issued a directive
under which 55 percent of the mined gold would be
bought by the state and 45 percent by the banks. But
even this was sneaky — it did not define the authorized
banks, did not abolish VAT and the special tax on
operations with precious metals, and did not allow gold
to be used as a surety in foreign trade operations. The
decree was thereby doomed to failure from the outset.

One of the options proposed by the Kolyma miners
is to organize a financial-industrial group which would
include all the oblast’s main producers — gold miners,
coal miners, power workers, and transportation workers.
Everyone understands the need to unite, since it is
utterly clear that if the gold-mining sector goes, others
will go too. This sad prospect makes even natural
monopolies — power workers and seamen — rein
in their appetites. This means that a real basis for
cooperation is emerging. Clearly, in such remote and
isolated territories as Magadan Oblast, this will be
virtually the only way to survive.

{(FBIS Translated Text] Kemerovo — ‘ne planned

and processing capacity while maintaining as many jobs
as possible for miners from shut-down enterprises will
be the policy of Russia’s Ministry of Fuel and Energy
in the next few years, sector Minister Yuriy Shafranik
Stated in the Kuzbass.

have already been built in the new industrial area.

The minister sees one other way of upgrading coal

proposed that a joint document on this problem be
drawn up.

Beloyarskaya AES Director Minimizes Sodium
Leak

LD2307204695 Moscew Russian Television Network
in Russian 1600 GMT 23 Jul 95

{From the "Vesti" newscast]

[FBIS Translated Text] Following the Chernobyl disas-
ter, any report of an incident at an atomic power station
[AES] is alarming. News of an accident at the Belo-
yarskaya AES was no exception. It put the inhabitants
of the nearby districts on their guard.

The AES director Oleg Makarovich Sarayev claims
no accident occurred. The incident — a leak of non-
radioactive sodium — is a routine breakdown that
occurs from time to time at stations of this sort and
cannot be classed as an accident. To avoid unpredictable
scenarios, the station was brought to a halt to deal with
the breakdown. The leak has now been eliminated. Unit
No. 3 of the Beloyarskaya AES is in start mode. There
are no deviations from the fixed working limits. The
radioactive situation in the atomic power workers’ town
of Zarechnyy corresponds to the normal work mode.

Incidentally, the BM-600 reactor, in operation since
1980, is one of the most reliable in Russia. It regulates
itself and closes itself down in the incredible event of a
failure in the accident protection measures.

[Begin recording] [O.M. Sarayev, director of Belo-
yarskaya AES] The single accident that occurred at
Chernobyl is not characteristic of either the state of
atomic power engineering nor the level of danger that
actually exists. [end recording]

{FBIS Transcribed Text] Moscow, July 21 (INTER-
FAX) — Russian President Boris Yeltsin signed a de-
cree on top-priority measures to improve Russia’s tax-
ation system Friday.

Commenting on the document in an interview with
INTERFAX, presidential aide on economics Aleksandr
Livshits emphasized the "instructive" character of the
decree, fully compatible with the existing legislation.

The decree was designed to improve the collection of
taxes, at the same time providing a "reduction in the
real tax burden,” he added.

Speaking on the main provisions of the decree, Livshits
pointed out the president’s instruction to the government
to amend the existing tax legislation to make the value-
added taxes variable to stimulate industrial production
and investments in it. The government was also asked
to cut the number of taxes.

Livshits remarked that the decree defined a procedure
for evaluating foreign trade deals in order to regulate
the taxation of barter contracts. Special task forces will
be created in tax departments to attend to taxpayers and
their complaints. At the same time, Russia’s penal code
will be amended to envision tougher penalties for tax
evasion. Livshits said the decree declared the October
1993 tax amnesty as nuli and void.

Yeltsin appointed First Deputy Prime Minister Anatoliy
Chubays as head of an ad hoc working group for
taxation reform.

Government To Fill Deficit Using Investment, Loans

LD2207172995 Moscow INTERFAX in English
1501 GMT 22 Jul 95

{FBIS Transcribed Text] Moscow, July 22 (INTER-
FAX) — Russia’s government plans to fill in next year’s
budget deficit with investment by the population and
with loans obtained from abroad, First Deputy Econom-
ics Minister Yakov Urinson said.

This will make it possible to avoid giving an impulse
to inflation, Urinson told INTERFAX.

The country’s population today has private savings of
between $25 billion and $30 billion and about 70 trillion
rubles, Urinson said. He said the government hoped to
obtain 50 trillion rubles from the population to cover
up more than half of the planned deficit of 85 trillion
rubles. The rest is to be closed with borrowings from
abroad.

Urinson also forecast a substantial increase in electricity
charges within the next two or three years, which, he
said, should entail use of energy-saving technologies
and change the general price structure nationwide.

The higher charges will primarily hit energy-intensive
industries, such as the aluminum industry, the deputy
minister said.

He said it would soon become one of the government’s
priority tasks to ensure a smooth and predictable in-
crease in electricity cnarges.

Deputy Premier Views Timeline for Joining GATT/
WTO

LD2107174095 Moscow INTERFAX in English
1536 GMT 21 Jul 95

{[FBIS Transcribed Text] Moscow, July 21 (INTER-
FAX) — "To join GATT/WTO [World Trade Organ-
ization] Russia may need from a year to two years,”
Russian Deputy Prime Minister, Minister for Foreign
Economic Relations Oleg Davydov has said.

RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS 45

“Everything will depend on how quickly Moscow
manages to coordinate the terms for its admission to
this international trade organization with all its 125
members," Davydov told INTERFAX in an exclusive
interview today after returning to Moscow from Geneva
yesterday evening.

In Geneva Davydov headed the Russian governmental
delegation at the first meeting of the working group for
Russia joining GATT/WTO.

Davydov said, "the changes that have happened to
the Russian economic mechanism over the last three
years, including the cancellation of central planning, the
abolishment of the state monopoly for foreign trade,
wide-scale privatization and price liberalization permit
Russia to declare its readiness to follow the multilateral
trade discipline within the scope of GATT/WTO."

Davydov said this point of view was shared by many
participants in the three-day talks in Geneva. He said the
U.S. representatives at the meeting had given Russia
especially important support by saying that the U.S.
fully approved of Russia’s candidacy for joining GATT/
WTO.

Davydov said that, however, to join this organization
Russia would have to make a lot of effort and agree
on many compromises. At the same time, he stressed
that the compromises would be achieved on the basis
of mutual concessions and would not run counter to the
national economy’s interests.

Davydov said he was confident that Russia’s joining
GATT/WTO was in the interests of everybody. He said
that for the organization’s members “Moscow will be-
come a Stable and predictable partner acting on the mar-
kets in line with the generally-recognized standards and
traditions." He said that for Russia its membership in
GATT/WTO “will remove the discriminatory obstacles
in trade and will let Russia become a full-fledged mem-
ber of the international economic community.”

Davydov said that Russia, as not a member of GATT/
WTO, was now suffering substantial losses in foreign
trade because it had to face numerous restrictions
regarding the access of its goods to the markets of this
organization’s members.

Davydov estimated these losses at about $1.5 billion a
year.
