Nazarbayev’s Thoughts on New Constitution

MM2107110995 Moscow ROSSIYSKAYA GAZETA
in Russian 2] Jul 95 pp 9,11

{Report by Yuriy Kirinitsiyanov: “Constitution of Pres-
idential Dimensions. It Seems Extremely Interesting
To Publish President Nazarbayev’s View on Some of
the Most Pressing Sections of the Kazakhstan Consti-
tution Being Drafted. Here Are the Head of State’s
Thoughts...."]

{[FBIS Translated Text] Almaty

On Article 40 from the ‘President’ Section

The Assembly of Peoples of Kazakhstan had a heated
discussion of the point of Article 40, which we included
at the suggestion of foreign experts.

Article 68 of the French Constitution and Article 49 of
the Greek Constitution affirm virtually the same thing:
“The president of the republic is not accountable for
actions committed in the performance of his duties
aside from cases of state treason.” When I have to
explain this provision I cite Article 45 of the draft,
which literally says the following: “Acts signed and
issued by the president of the Kazakh [Kazakhskaya]
Republic are ratified accordingly by the signature of the
prime minister, the chairman of each of the chambers
of parliament, or other senior officials who are assigned
legal responsibility for the constitutionality and legality
of these acts.”

Thus I would like to stress that no one is trying to
relieve the president of his burden of responsibility.
He is responsible for the country’s territorial integrity
and for people's civil rights. But at the same time
developed democratic countries do not summon the
president to the Constitutional Court to provide any kind
of explanations. But when the discussion arose I decided
to remove this point — Article 45 “covers” it.

..On the Current Constitution

I have never previously claimed that the previous
Constitution was the best. On the contrary it is the
‘product” of compromise between the former Union
parliament and the realities facing our country. But it
does not befit the head of state, who is obliged under
the Constitution to ensure the observance of laws and
rights, to criticize the country’s Fundamental Law by
which it is living today. What option do ordinary voters
have then?

On the contrary, both the president and all citizens
should obey the Constitution that is in force.

This report may contain copynghted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copynght owners

FBIS-SOV-95-141
24 July 1995

..On Changes in the New Constitution

Our life is extremely dynamic, every day brings some-
thing new. Therefore this document cannot be viewed as
something cast in stone. At the same time I am opposed
to partial, hasty changes to the text. If the people vote
for the Constitution they should also decide whether it
should be changed.

The president is given the right to submit amendments
— either for national debate or to the parliament, where
a decision is adopted by a three-fourths majority vote.

New generations will come along and with them new
realities. The 21st century is coming. Things will
certainly change. But I am convinced that the fabric
of the document will remain. Note, the U.S. Bill of
Rights has been in existence for over 200 years. But
amendments to it fill several dozen volumes. And that
is quite normal.

..On the Status of the Capital

I can firmly state that I intend to move the capital to
Aqmola. It is necessary to be utterly consistent here. Our
state’s Constitution does not name the capital. Why?
Because only the USSR Constitution contains an article
of this kind. I think that it would be correct to adopt
a special law on this point; moreover it should also

stipulate the status of the capital. I think that we will
also determine the status of Almaty once the capital has
moved.

..On the Policy of Neutrality

I think that there is no need for Kazakhstan to separately
declare itself to be a nuclear-free state. That is acknowl-
edged by the rest of the world. However, Article 8 reads
as follows: "The Republic of Kazakhstan, respecting the
universally recognized principles and norms of interna-
tional law, pursues a policy of cooperation and good-
neighborly relations between states, a policy based on
their equality, noninterference in each other’s internal
affairs, and the peaceful resolution of international dis-
putes.” Point 2 of this article says: “The Republic of
Kazakhstan renounces war and the first use of armed
force and aspires to a policy of neutrality.” | was on the
whole inclined to declare Kazakhstan a neutral state, but
treaties signed within the CIS framework do not enable
me to do so.

However, when concluding new treaties we must bear
in mind the people’s desire for neutrality. We have
no intention of fighting anyone. We would like our
neighbors near and far to treat us in the same way.
And another point: I make no secret of the fact that
I take a negative view of France's decision to resume

CENTRAL ASIA 71

nuclear testing, and of the explosions being carried out
by China....

..«Once More On Changes to the Text of the New
Constitution

I have always considered Kazakhstan a state for all
citizens regardless of nationality, religion, and political
convictions. The text of the previous Constitution said
that this is the state of the “self-determined Kazakh
people.” This idea also caused me some embarrassment
when I started explaining it. After all,. my signature is
on the Fundamental Law....

A second, equally acute question is that of land own-
ership. A very difficult problem. The sociological polls
that I have already mentioned were needed. We propose
that our economic policy be seen as moving toward a
market economy, and that is why the draft laws have
adopted radical changes on the private ownership of
land. Let me cite Article 5: “Land, its mineral wealth,
water, the vegetable and animal worlds, and other nat-
ural resources are in state ownership but may also be
in private ownership on a basis, conditions, and within
limits prescribed by law.”

..On Borrowing Foreign Experience

I am sometimes reproached for the “one-sided appear-
ance” of the Constitution. But people overlook the fact
that those sections which ban or do not permit some-
thing do not contain a single paragraph or proposal
drawn up by Nazarbayev personally. We availed our-
selves of the experience of the constitutional system in
France, Germany (West), Italy, Greece.... No one would
dare to say that these countries are undemocratic.

However, I will not hide the fact that there is a great
desire to take account of our realities, our Asian or, if
you like, Oriental mentality, the life of Kazakhstanis
and Kazakhs, their past, and their traditions. But all that
must be linked to our movement toward a democratic
state based on the rule of law.

..On the Main Aim of the New Constitution

Once a discussion has ended, it takes time to analyze and
summarize all the proposals received. I reckon that the
date of a national referendum will be announced in early
August. I will ask the people of Kazakhstan to support
me. I will ask journalists not to call it “"Nazarbayev’s"
Constitution. | am just one of the authors, and mv real
objective is not to establish a regime based on personal
power but stability in the state. The Constitution of
Kazakhstan must protect its citizens from the bloody
wrangling which we can see in certain Commonwealth
countries.

This report may contain copyrighted materia). Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners

72 CENTRAL ASIA

..On the State and Official Languages

I believe that the Russian language should be given
official status. That would be fair in our conditions. I
consulted Russian citizens on this along with Patriarch
Aleksiy II of Moscow and all Russia. And I met with
complete understanding.

Removing the Kazakh language’s status as the state
language would be humiliating for the Kazakhs. We will
not be able to do that. Nor would that add to stability
in the republic....

The Russian language currently has the unfathomable
status of the “language of interethnic relations.” It will
now be an official language, i.e. as translated from
the Latin “the official government [pravitelstvennyy,
dolzhnostnoy] language”.... I believe that is normal. I
really do ask representatives of the mass media not to
enable anyone to speculate on this matter.

Engineer on Possibilities of Saving Aral

954F2824A Almaty KAZAKHSTANSKAYA PRAVDA
in Russian 15 Jul 95 p4

{Article by Ivan Druzhinin, engineer: “Loss of Aral
a Foregone Conclusion—lIs It Conscious and Deliber-
ate?” ]

{FBIS Translated Text] Earlier than the word "Cher-
nobyl,” the word “Aral” came to denote an alarm, warn-
ing people not to succumb to the dangerous temptation
to take everything possible from nature, be unconcerned
about preserving it, and ignore its objective laws. In vir-
tually a single generation, literally in front of everyone’s
eyes, the largest lake on the planet (not without reason
people respectfully termed it a sea), a lake that emerged
more than 2 million years ago, has almost ceased to ex-
ist.

Can it be saved?

In the assessment of independent experts from the
United States, Japan, and Canada, about $200 billion
will be required to stop the Arai Sea from drying up
and to restore it to its former level. There presently exist
over 200 projects for saving the Aral, but none of them
can even begin to be implemented—the treasury lacks
the money.

In response to the proposal of the executive board of the
International Foundation for Saving the Aral that plans
be submitted to them for competitive judging, I decided
to do my part in the noble cause of rescuing the Aral
and submitted my own plan.

Its essence consists in the fact that stability of the
existing volume of water in the Aral can be secured in
1996 and maintained for subsequent years. The natural
water runoff from the Amu Darya and Syrdarya will
provide the Aral 10 cubic kilometers per year, drainage
and reservoir water-—14 cubic kilometers a year. We
can obtain 6 cubic kilometers a year in the gravity
overflow from mountain lakes through existing river
beds (present reserves of such waters amount to 500
cubic kilometers), and 5 cubic kilometers a year from
atmospheric precipitation. Some 33 cubic kilometers a
year would be required to stabilize the sea at this time,
and the measures enumerated above will yield 35 cubic
kilometers a year.

As far as restoring the sea to its former level (+53
meters) is concerned, my plan envisages the use of
subsurface waters of the Kara-Kum in a volume of 32
cubic meters a year. To do this, I am proposing a new

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

FBIS-SOV-95-141
24 July 1995

water intake method with respect to subsurface waters—
the cutting of underground irrigation tunnels (drainage
galleries or culverts). Iran is the country where we see
classic use of irrigation tunnels. The same conditions of
nature found in Iran are found in Turkmenistan, where
it is planned to use the subsurface Kara-Kum waters
to save the Aral. Desert occupies a large portion of
the territory of these countries. There is practically no
river runoff, no volume of precipitation. However, Iran
has provided subsurface water to its entire populace—
20 million people, and to 50 million head of livestock.
Hundreds of cubic meters of subsurface water are used
each year to irrigate large tracts of land as well.

The volume of subsurface Kara-Kum water (32 cubic
kilometers a year) taken to save the Aral will not upset
the water balance here (this aspect is treated in detail
in the plan). In accordance with the plan, water from
32 irrigation tunnels, each 1 kilometer long, will feed
into the Sarykamys storage facility, raise the water level
therein, and then flow by gravity through the existing
dry bed of the Daryalyk to the Amu Darya, then on to
the Aral Sea.

The cost of cutting afd setting up an irrigation tunnel 1
kilometer long is R300,000 [rubles]. The entire project
will cost R9.5 million (in 1988 prices). If it turns out
during the process of engineering surveys that the water
discharge will be less than 32 cubic meters a year, it will
be necessary to increase the number of tunnels or the
length of each of them.

The level of subsoil waters of the Kara-Kum has in-
creased sharply in recent times. Large areas of solon-
chak have formed in the summertime from the intense
evaporation of water. This presents a new ecological
nuisance.

Corresponding member of the Turkmenistan Academy
of Sciences V. Kunin asserts, “If we were to mentally
remove the rock mass and lay bare the mirror of subsoil
waters of the Kara-Kum, we would be presented with
the vision of an unimaginable sea of water, extending
from the Amu Darya River westward and linking with
the mirror of waters of the Caspian Sea."

Consequently, if the level of subsurface Kara-Kum
waters rises, the level of water in the Caspian Sea
will also rise. This is why coastal settlements there are
presently undergoing flooding.

An alternative for saving the Aral has been proposed
which involves construction of a 500-meter channel in
the sands from the Caspian Sea to the Aral. Like all
the other alternatives, this is a utopian concept. If water
came in from the Caspian Sea to the Aral, it would
constitute an ordinary pollutant. The salt saturation of

CENTRAL ASIA 73

the southeastern portion of the Caspian Sea is high,
and the salt concentration in the Aral is higher still.
Consequently, in a few years the Aral Sea would only
be suitable for the extraction of salt. And the salt water
of the Caspian would ruin the fresh waters of the Kara-
Kum. Apparently, the author of this plan failed to take
into account the fact that the Aral is a lake with no
outflow—it can be saved solely with fresh water.

The plan I proposed for rescuing the Aral was examined
by the executive board of the International Foundation
for Saving the Aral at the end of 1994 and by the Kazakh
Academy of Agricultural Sciences, which provided a
conclusion assessing the plan as interesting, attractive,
and deserving of special attention. Unfortunately, the
above-mentioned organizations failed to undertake any
practical measures whatsoever related to saving the Aral
Sea.

We can still save the Aral, but only under one
condition—if we cease the endless chatter and inaction.
We must save the Aral! It was noted at a conference
of the international public committee Aral—Asia—
Kazakhstan that numerous commissions, foundations,
and departments are taking advantage of the Aral
tragedy to secure their personal financial enrichment
and are doing absolutely nothing to save it.

To date, all these organizations have tried to explain that
it is impossible to save the Aral, since the $200 billion
required to do so is lacking. But if a plan is on hand
enabling us to accomplish this for $10 million, why is
it not being considered?

country and believe that the move would be to the

Uzbekistan

‘Breakthrough’ in Uzbek-U.S. Ties Noted

MM2107135595 Moscow ROSSIYSKAYA GAZETA
in Russian 21 Jul95 pll

[Article by Vakhtang Sheliya: “Tashkent’s Appeal for
Washington. United States Intends To Provide Uzbek-
istan With Every Kind of Assistance" — from the “De-
partmental Supplement” section]

[FBIS Transiated Text] We have gotten accustomed to
all the republics of the former USSR striving to win
support, primarily material support, from the West and
the United States in the implementation of their reforms.
They do this in different ways. Some state leaders
cannot make a move without foreign advisers, while
others surrender whole industries to foreign companies
to manage, and yet others regularly ask for money.

Regrettably, a style of relations has been established
between Russia and the West whereby aid for us is
generally associated with political concessions in inter-
national relations and domestic policy. This virtually

FBIS-SOV-95-141
24 July 1995

always does palpable damage to Russia’s national in-
terests.

The history of relations between the United States and
Uzbekistan may serve as an example of how success can
be secured in foreign policy and translated into material
advantage for the state while at the same time preserving
its interests and own dignity.

This July will mark a year since the intensive develop-
ment of Uzbek-U.S. relations began. In 1994, as a result
of three rounds of political consultations, commonality
of interests and proximity of approaches on problems
of global and regional security, cooperation within the
framework of international organizations, the deve'op-
ment of trade and economic relations, and also o1 the
prevailing situation in Tajikistan and Afghanistan ‘vere
placed on record.

Political relations between the two countries have
opened up opportunities for contacts in other spheres.
The greatest progress has been made in the sphere of
trade and economic relations. The United States has
granted Uzbekistan preferential trade status, two joint
political and commercial risk insurance companies have
been set up, and it has been decided to open a branch
of the U.S.-Central Asian Enterprise Support Fund and
a U.S. business center in Tashkent.

Around 10 branches of U.S. firms have opened in
Uzbekistan and over 100 Uzbek-U.S. enterprises are
in operation. The Uzbek National Ba « for Foreign
Economic Activity has established coope:2tion with a
number of U.S. banks, including Chemical Bank, J.P.
Morgan Corporation, etc.

A major Uzbek-U.S. gold extraction joint venture
“Zaravshan-Newmont" was commissioned in May
This venture is one of the largest in the CIS in terms of
U.S. investment. It has been agreed to build a major gas
compressor station in Uzbekistan. Some $200 million
will be allocated for its construction, $140 million of
which will come from the United States in the form of
targeted loans.

Thus it can be said with complete justification today that
there has been a breakthrough in relations between the
two countries. This was preceded by a long period (1991
through 1994) without proper mutual understanding be-
tween the sides, particularly on questions of domestic
policy in Uzbekistan. To put it more simply, Washing-
ton would like Uzbekistan to authorize the activity of
Opposition parties and relax its control over the pro-
cesses under way in society. At one time James Baker
personally tried to persuade Islam Karimov to ease up
on the opposition. At the same time hints were made to
Tashkent that if it followed the West's advice it would

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners

FBIS-SOV-95-141
24 July 1995

immediately receive support from the United States and
other developed countries and a generous flow of cash.

However Uzbekistan did not succumb to Western
politicians’ blandishments and pressure or to pressure
in the Western and Russian mass media. People in
Tashkent did what they thought necessary, And Uzbek-
istan ultimately emerged the victor.

The results of Uzbekistan’s economic development to
date confirm that the Uzbek model of development
is viable. Uzbekistan’s economic indicators today are
among the best in the CIS.

Thus, for instance, on the basis of the results of 1994
production only fell by 4 percent and GDP fell by
3.5 percent. Industrial production stood at 101 percent
compared with the previous year. At the same time
growth was recorded in the oil extraction, gas, coal,
and machine building industries: The figures were
112.1, 120, 100.4, and 110.3 percent respectively. Light-
industry production stood at 107.9 percent. Agricultural
gross output stood at 96.7 percent, and the budget deficit
represents 3.5 percent of GDP.

During the first quarter of the year the pace of develop-
ment was sustained. Industrial production stood at 83.5
percent. While the figures for the oil extraction, gas,

machine building and metalworking, and light industries
were 107.9, 113.6, 102.7, and 97.3 percent respectively.
The figure for gross agricultural output was 100.6 per-
cent. The budget deficit is 1.3 percent of GDP.

The rate of inflation in January, March, April, and May
was as follows: 16, 7.8, 7, and 6.5 percent respectively.
Despite the fact that prices of petroleum products were
deregulated early in the year.

There is every indication that Washington has concluded
that maintaining stability in a state in a complex region
like Central Asia requires tough measures, and West
European concepts of human rights are not always
applicable to local conditions.

According to Western and U.S. experts, a favorable
climate for investors has developed in Uzbekistan on the
whole. The IMF, the European Bank for Reconstruction
and Development, and Western countries and the United
States have made over $1 billion in capital investments.
However, it is not a question of state loans (incidentally
Uzbekistan has no foreign debt). It is being allocated
money for specific, promising projects — for the
opening up of oil fields, the modernization of oil
refineries, the construction of automobile plants, etc.
Unlike many other CIS member states Uzbekistan is
able to provide investors with political guarantees. The
chosen model for the state’s domestic system inspires

CENTRAL ASIA 77

confidence that investing money in Uzbekistan is a
reliable, promising process.

The United States attaches particular importance to the
fact that Uzbekistan, with its geographical proximity to
such trouble spots as Afghanistan, is retaining its inter-
nal stability. The United States thinks that Uzbekistan
is in a fairly strong position to play a role in settling
these conflicts.

However, Uzbekistan’s prospects of counterbalancing
Iran hold the greatest appeal for Washington. This is
illustrated by statements by U.S. officials, in particular
Defense Secretary William Perry. Mindful of this in-
terest and the stable progress being made by reforms
in Uzbekistan one can predict massive all-around U.S.
support for Uzbekistan, which does of course open up
major prospects for Tashkent.

This support is timely for Uzbekistan. It will be con-
ducive to the strengthening of its leading positions in
the region and will enable it to switch to market re-
lations with the minimum possible losses. At the same
time I must stress that the promised aid is not dependent
upon any conditions, as is the case for the United States
in its relations with Russia, for instance. This marks a
major, quite deserved success for Uzbek diplomacy.

Special Service Denies Attack Against Karimov
MK2107105895 Moscow KOMMERSANT-DAILY
in Russian 2] Jul 95 p4

[Politics Desk report: "Details of Attempt on Uzbekistan
President's Life. Nobody Will Ever Know Anything")

[FBIS Translated Text!) KOMMERSANT DAILY has
already reported that there was an attempt on the life
of Uzbekistan President Islam Karimov at 2110 local
time [in Tashkent]. Yesterday, the editors learned some
details of what happened indicating that the action had
been planned by people who are far from amateurs.
According to some information, one of the shooters was
held by the National Security Service [NSS].

In the phrase of Uzbekistan Foreign Ministry head
Abdulaz Kamilov, “the contact of the car’s windshield
with hard objects moving at a great speed” took place as
the president's motorcade was moving along a special
road extending practically half way across the city.
Karimov was heading home. There was firing from
a roof or the roofs of high-rise buildings adjacent to
the road. Experts’ conclusions about the nature of the
traces left on the armored windshield by the impact
of two “hard objects” makes it possible to confidently
claim that use was made of a weapon more powerful
than regulation army weaponry. Thus, this refutes the
version that this was the action of an amateur loner: only
professionals have access to the arms used — notably,
those who serve or served in special subunits. It is,
however, impossible to obtain more precise information
bout what the Uzbek special services already know
— they even doubt the very fact there was an attack,
explaining that it has been forbidden for them to have
contacts with the press on this affair.

However, KOMMERSANT-DAILY has learned from
confidential sources that the president's car was
shot at from two points. One of the snipers (the
KOMMERSANT-DAILY informants used precisely

is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners

this term) was apprehended minutes after the attack.
The second one slipped away. The prosecutors office
is conducting an investigation, but the information on
how it is proceeding is also being withheld. A solid

that the public will never know either the results of the
investigation or what is going to happen to the arrested

person (if there is one). One proof of this is the latest
Statement from the NSS Press Service, which boils

Turkey. - IF).

because Yerevan denies passage to people and cargoes
into the enclave across its territory.

The Armenian diplomat recalled that from the first days
of the Karabakh conflict Iran had declared the readiness
to offer its territory to transport cargoes and fuels from
Azerbaijan to Nakhichevan.

The source said that similar proposals were made by
Armenia. "Yerevan even offered Nakhichevan a certain
amount of electricity for the transit of gas and fuel oil
from Iran for Armenian thermal power plants. However,
Baku ignored all of our proposals”, the diplomat said.

Strengthening the defence capability of the two

Further on Bagratyan’s Reappointment

LD2307191995 Moscow ITAR-TASS in English
1826 GMT 23 Jul 95

[By Tigran Liloyan]}

{[FBIS Transcribed Text] YEREVAN, July 23 (ITAR-
TASS) — Armenian President Levon Ter-Petrosyan
signed a decree on Sunday reappointing Grant
Bagratyan as the prime minister.

The president also asked Bagratyan to submit his
proposals on the make-up of a new government within
three days.

Under Article 5 of the Armenian Constitution adopted
in the July 5 nation-wide referendum, the government
consists of the prime minister and ministers. The
positions of vice-prime ministers and state ministers
have been abolished. The heads of the state committees
have also been removed from the government.

The new composition of the government is expected
to be announced by July 27 when the parliament will
convene for its first session.

Observers do not predict radical changes in the Cabinet.
They believe that Interior Minister Vanik Siradegyan
and Foreign Minister Vahan Papazyan will retain their
positions. Vazgen Sarkisyan is likely to be appointed the
republic’s defence minister. The Security Service may
also be reorganised.

Bagratyan was born in 1958 in Yerevan. In 1979,
he graduated cum laude from Yerevan Institute of
the National Economy and in 1987 he defended a
dissertation in economics.

CAUCASUS 81

In 1990, Bagratyan was appointed as first vice-premier
and head of the State Economics Committee. In Febru-
ary 1993, he was named to be the prime minister.

Bagratyan is a member of the ruling Armenian National
Movement. He is married and has a son. The premier
has a command of Russian, English and German.

In the opinion of the Azeri president, the forthcoming

Novruzov Discusses Joint Border Defense With
Russia

NC2107160295 Istanbul ZAMAN in Turkish

17 Jul95 » 4

{Interview with General Abbasali Novruzov, deputy
head of the National Intelligence Department and com-
mander of the border guards, by ZAMAN correspon-
dents Osman Sonmez and Zeynel Abidin in Baku; date
not given]

{FBIS Translated Text] Baku — [ZAMAN] There ts an
argument about Azerbaijan cooperating with Russia to
defend its border, despite the disclosures that President
Heydar Aliyev and you have made to the effect that
Azerbaijan will defend its own borders.” Can you
clarify this issue?

[Novruzov] Azerbaijan is an independent country and
it will defend its borders. We have not signed several
of the documents that were drawn up during the CIS
summit in Minsk on various issues, particularly on
the joint defense of the CIS borders. They conflict
with our country’s independence. Moscow claimed that
Azerbaijan was unable to defend its border when it
made its proposal on joint defense. It claimed that
Azerbaijan cannot resist the provocateurs in Iran and
Turkey. I asked whether any provocateur has been
identified or arrested. My question went unanswered
lat was because the claim was unfounded.

FBIS-SOV-95-141
24 July 1995

Nevertheless, Russia has signed agreements with several
other former Soviet republics on the defense of their
borders. Azerbaijan has concluded agreements with Iran
and Turkey on our common borders. However, it has not
done so with Russia. An agreement must definitely be
concluded between the two independent countries.

[ZAMAN] Does Azerbaijan have the resources it needs
to defend its borders?

[Novruzov] Yes, it does. We have the strength we need
to defend our borders. It is true that we have technical
problems, but they will be resolved.

[ZAMAN] Russia proposed in 1994 to help Azerbaijan
by installing technical equipment at the border and
training cadres for it. Observers say that Russia will use
that as an opportunity to deploy its military personnel in
Azerbaijan. That will be the first concession it will get
from your republic on the defense of the CIS borders.

[Novruzov] That is an erroneous and baseless hypoth-
esis. Russia has supplied us with barbed wire, simple
technical equipment, and military material in accordance
with the agreement we signed in 1994. All that mate-
rial was earmarked for the defense of our border. We
have the personnel we need to use the material we have
received from that country. We do noi need to invite
anyone to help us. The most important aspect of the
agreement we reached was that the material we will re-
ceive from Russia for border defense will be exempt
from taxes. Unfortunately, that has remained only on
paper.

[ZAMAN] Can we assume that Azerbaijan will be
able to defend its border if its technical problems are
resolved?

{[Novruzov] The former Soviet Union had modern
technical equipment. It used extraordinary systems to
defend its border. Nevertheless, it collapsed. It did
not help stop its disintegration. The defense of our
border with barbed wire is a provisional measure until
Stability is established. The borders of the developed
countries are marked with symbolic lines. We hope that
an imaginary line will mark our border in the future.

{ZAMAN] Can you comment on the problems the short-
age of technical equipment has caused for Azerbaijan’s
border guards?

[Novruzov] The war that has continued for seven years
has weakened Azerbaijan economically. Our people are
forced to seek employment in Iran and Turkey. Mean-
while, the Mafia groups have intensified their drug traf-
ficking activities. We have seized narcotic substances
from foreign countries at our border several times. We

This report may contain copynghted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners

FBIS-SOV-95-141
24 July 1995

are not very experienced in that field. Furthermore, we
are short of modern technical equipment.

[ZAMAN] Can Russia’s stand 1 the Caspian Sea’s
Status be linked with Moscow’s proposal for the joint
defense of the CIS borders?

[Novruzov] No, I do not think so. The Caspian Sea
was divided into several sectors during the Soviet era.
Azerbaijan extracts oil in its own sector. No serious
differences on territorial waters have emerged between
Azerbaijan and Russia.

Trilateral Peacekeeping Forces To Remain in
Ossetia

LD2107142995 Tbilisi IPRINDA in Russian
1230 GMT 21 Jul 95

{[FBIS Translated Text] Tskhinvali, 21 Jul — An
IPRINDA correspondent reports that a quadripartite
Joint Control Commission on settling the Georgian-
Ossetian conflict, which includes representatives of
Georgia, Russia, North Ossetia and the Tskhinvali
region, concluded its work on 20 July. Two issues

were on the agenda of the Tskhinvali session of the
commission: the stay of a joint peacekeeping contingent
in the region and the status of the Tskhinvali region.

The commander of the joint peacekeeping forces, Ma-
jor General Anatoliy Merkulyev says it is necessary that
the amount of armaments in the peacekeepers’ posses-
sion should be reduced. At the same time, speaking
of material and technical supplies for the peacekeeping
forces, he advises that helicopters and at least 10 cross-
country vehicles be provided for patrolling the area. He
also informed the session about a so-called cleansing
of the personnel of the Ossetian, Georgian, and Rus-
sian peacekeeping battalions — servicemen, who were
involved in combat activities during the conflict, are
being discharged. The Georgian “blue helmets” have
already been “cleansed;" 36 people will be discharged
from the Ossetian battalion and three servicemen will
be discharged from the Russian battalion. According to
the general, the process of confiscating arms from the
local population is extremely slow.

A proposition was voiced st the session to have the
Georgian and Ossetian peacekeeping battalions dis-
banded and have only the Russian one retained. The
proposition was opposed by Vyacheslav Gabarayev,
chairman of the South Ossetian Council of Ministers
He said the joint forces had to remain there until the
Status of South Ossetia was specified.

Rescue Corps Goes Nonpolitical; Rejects
Mkhedrioni

LD2207180495 Moscow ITAR-TASS World Service
in Russian 1132 GMT 22 Jul 95

[By ITAR-TASS correspondent Albert Kochetkov]

{[FBIS Translated Text} Tbilisi, 22 Jul (ITAR-TASS) —
Georgia’s Rescue Corps is outside politics and forms
part of the republic’s structure of government, Longi
Chelidze, the agency's first deputy chairman, told a
news conference here today

Asked by ITAR-TASS about his agency's relationship
with the Mkhedrioni paramilitary opposition, which
turned itself into a political movement a few days ago,
he clearly distanced himself from that organization,
although he did confirm that there are large numbers
of Mkhedrioni members within the Rescue Corps.

Long: Chelidze acknowledged that some of them have
been charged with serious crimes but, he thought, the
crimes are far fewer than those committed by Georgia's
Ministry of Internal Affairs, which has had to set up a
separate corrective labor colony for those convicted.

It was also explained to the media that the Rescue Corps
now performs the duties of the republic's former civil

CAUCASUS 87

defense system, and its structure is very similar to that
of the Russian Emergencies Ministry. The Georgian
Government has yet to endorse the Corps’ standing
orders, however.

Yet the Rescue Corps’ past is closely linked to that
of the Mkhedrioni. It was formed from armed units
of the Mkhedrioni, which themselves were formed
by parliamentary Deputy Jaba loseliani, a 68-year-old
doctor of philology and one of the leaders of the January
1992 overthrow of Zviad Gamsakhurdia. Ioseliani was
charged with grave crimes in his youth and, according
to some observers, to this day wields considerable
influence in the underworld.

The Mkhedrioni initially said their aim was to rehabili-
tate former convicts into society, but they later became
a potent poiitical force whose representatives today oc-
cupy senior posts within the machinery of state.

At the same time, there is a growing public perception of
the Rescue Corps as a criminal outfit akin to the Mafia
This perception is rooted in a series of major campaigns
by the law enforcement authorities against organized
crime that led to the detention of numerous Mkhedrioni
members within the Rescue Corps on suspicion of
serious crimes.

LD2007121695 Tallinn Radio Tallinn Network
in Russian 0600 GMT 20 Jul 95

[FBIS Translated Text] Estonia does not support the
position of Daniel Tarschys, secretary general of the
Council of Europe, who thinks Russia could be given
full membership in the Council of Europe in January or
February 1996. Andra Veidemann, head of the Estonian
delegation to the Council of Europe Parliamentary
Assembly, has stated this.

Veidemann added that the Legal Affairs and Hu-
man Rights Committee of the Parliamentary Assem-
bly, members of which recently visited Chechnya, has
pointed out in a report that it cannot see any possi-
bility of giving Russia membership in the Council of
Europe in the near future. Veidemann also said that Es-
tonia would prefer Ukraine to gain membership before
Russia. According to Veidemann, the Parliamentary As-
sembly of the Council of Europe will discuss Ukraine’s
membership at its autumn session this year.

Latvia

President, Premier Meet Interior Ministry Officials

WS2107114795 Riga LETA in English
0925 GMT 2] Jul 95

{[FBiS Transcribed Text] Jurmala, July 21 LETA —
The President of Latvia, Guntis Ulmanis, and the
Prime Minister, Maris Gailis, addressed the meeting of
senior Officials form the Interici Ministry of Latvia at
their semi-annual meeting in the town of Jurmala on
Thursday, July 20.

President Ulmanis considers the crime situation in
Latvia as critical. He said that at present it is only
a guess that there are considerable money laundering
operations going on in Latvia. To obtain proof and
to control these operations the officials of the Interior
Ministry will have to work closely with the defense
forces, especially the National Voluntary Guard. Closer
cooperation should improve the situation substantially,
said the president.

Prime Minister Gailis noted that all the organized
crime structures in Latvia are wellknown. The next
step must be the isolation of these structures from
society. Mr. Gailis proposes to call a special session
of the Saeima (parliament) of Latvia in order to pass
the needed legislation. The participants of the meeting
welcomed Mr. Gailis’ announcement that everybody,
without exception, is equal under the law.

Interior Ministry Denies Rumors of Banking
Collapse

LD2107134195 Riga Radio Riga Network in Latvian
1200 GMT 21 Jul 95

(FBIS Translated Text] The press center of the Ministry
of Internal Affats of the Republic of Latvia has issued
a Statement that says that the banking crisis has recently
given rise to groundless rumors about a collapse of
the Latvian banking system. Rumors are being spread
to both the Ministry of Finance and the Ministry of
Internal Affairs about possible bankruptcies of Rigas
Komercbanka, Pareks Banka, Vacijas-Latvijas Banka,
and Zemes Banka. In spite of the crisis and company

their final aim is to create panic in society. The sources

under the procedure provided for by the law.

Lithuania
IMF Officials Concerned About Rumors of
Devaluation

LD2007132495 Vilnius Radio Vilnius International
Service in Lithuanian 2100 GMT 19 Jul 95

[FBIS Translated Text] Now that the stabilization of
Lithuania’s macroeconomy has been achieved, Lithua-
nia should continue to be guided by those principles
of economic management on which the Lithuanian and
IMF three-year program is based — according to a re-
port by a Lithuanian Bank spokesman, such a point of
view was voiced -. the sitting held on 14 July by the
executive directors of the IMF.

According to the report, the IMF executive directors
said that Lithuania is one of the most progressive
among countries with transitional economies and the
first among the Baltic states which has signed the three-
year program financed by the Extended Fund [as heard].
The fact that Lithuania has managed to reduce inflation
to single digits was described as a positive achievement
at the sitting.

The IMF executive directors voiced their concern about
rumors on the devaluation of the litas. According to the
IMF economists, the actual exchange rate of the litas
is increasing. According to some directors, the nominal
quotation of that litas should be changed, because this
would increase confidence in the Lithuanian currency
leading to decreases in inflation.

As already reported, the IMF spoke favorably about
economic reforms in Lithuania and allotted an additional
$32.2 million loan.

Parliamentarians Refused Visas To Visit Chechnya
LD2107113295 Vilnius Radio Vilnius Network
in Lithuanian 0900 GMT 21 Jul 95

(FBIS Translated Text] Russia has refused entry visas
to Lithuanian parliamentarians wishing to travel to

FBIS-SOV-95-141
24 July 1995

Chechnya and the neighboring regions, although the
formalities requested by the Russian Embassy have been
Cafried out.

Seimas members Algirdas Endriukaitis and Romualdas
Ozolas requested the Russian Embassy in Vilnius at
the beginning of July to grant them visas for a visit to
Groznyy. However, they were refused. Instead, it was
suggested that they could visit other regions of Russia,
although entry visas for visiting neighboring Ingushetia
were also refused. The grounds given for this were that
the original invitation document and not a faxed version
was needed.

Algirdas Endriukaitis, leader of the international parlia-
mentary group on the Chechnya problem, said that on
17 July, the original invitation letter from President Rus-
lan Aushev of Ingushetia, inviting the Lithuanian MPs
to visit Nazran, was presented to the Russian Embassy
in Vilnius. However, officials at the Russian Embassy
in Vilnius told the Lithuanian MPs that there had been
no reply from Moscow and that most likely there would
not be one.

Russian Embassy Explains Violations of Airspace

LD2107133695 Vilnius Radio Vilnius Network
in Lithuanian 1100 GMT 21 Jul 95

[FBIS Translated Text] The Foreign Ministry of the Re-
public of Lithuania received a reply from ths Embassy
of the Russian Federation on the violation of Lithuanian
airspace that occurred on 22 and 23 June.

The Russian Embassy noted that because of poor
meteorological conditions and not enough fuel for the
aircraft to return to a Russian airport, the captains of
the two AN-12 aircraft of the Russian Federation Baltic
Air Force made an unauthorized decision to violate
Lithuanian airspace.

The note says that the culprits have been punished and
appropriate measures taken by the Russian Federation
Defense Ministry so violations of this nature do not
occur again in the future.
